# LEDGER (append-only)

Claims and status changes, failed approaches with root causes, corrections, integrity events. Never edit an earlier entry; append a correction that references it.

## 2026-10-30 · integrity event · CI judge defined
- `.github/workflows/lean.yml` created: job `work` builds the `Work` library (sorry allowed); job `checked` builds `Checked` with axiom-audit (allowlist propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound), leanchecker and nanoda (sorry not allowed).
- Status: UNTESTED. Must pass positive controls (annex 39.6) before any result is called PROVED.

## 2026-10-30 · governance · annex v3.0 → v3.1
- Added 40.0 (full fidelity audit applies to headline statements and definitions, not helper lemmas), a results-first preference in 42.1, "Physlib formatting only when preparing a contribution" in 47.1, and LIII (pace and accountability: 2-run target pick, 3-run first milestone, daily review, 24-run stall rule, short records).
- Reason: Alexander asked for a review aimed at results, not strategy. These cut audit and formatting overhead and add deadlines that turn 12 runs a day into results.
- Added CONSTITUTION_COMPLETE.md (all parts in one file) and governance/SHA256SUMS.

## 2026-10-30 · integrity event · lake-manifest.json added
- First CI run (40965992094) failed before building: lean-action requires `lake-manifest.json`, and Lean could not be run locally to generate it.
- Fix: copied Physlib's manifest (same toolchain, Mathlib v4.35.0 at 56a005855ea17e0b0958f73ec801ae140b0a1679, which matches the v4.35.0 tag; all other packages are Mathlib's own pinned dependencies) and renamed the package. No check was changed.

## 2026-10-30 · integrity event · nanoda moved to own step
- CI run 40966121805: `work` green; `checked` built and passed leanchecker, then lean-action's built-in nanoda step failed because it could not detect a module name from our lakefile (it expects a `[package]` table and exports the package-named module). axiom-audit was skipped as a result.
- Fix (commit b4dbb86): nanoda disabled inside lean-action and run by `scripts/nanoda_checked.sh` on module `Checked`, same tools and settings as lean-action v1.7.0 (permitted axioms incl. Lean.trustCompiler, non-hard error). nanoda acts as the independent type checker; axiom-audit remains the axiom judge with the strict allowlist. No check weakened relative to the intended design.

## 2026-10-30 · integrity event · nanoda report-only (known gap)
- CI run 40966663174: lean-action step green, including leanchecker and axiom-audit ("audited 1 declaration(s) under 'Checked'; all within the allowlist"). nanoda failed with "invalid digit found in string" while parsing the 13.8M-line export: the current lean4export output format and nanoda_lib's `debug` branch don't match for Lean v4.35.0. This is a tooling incompatibility, not a problem with our proofs; lean-action's own nanoda step would hit it too.
- Change: nanoda step set to `continue-on-error` (report-only). Blocking checks are now: build, leanchecker (kernel replay), axiom-audit (strict allowlist), forbidden-token grep.
- Open: repair nanoda (e.g. pin a lean4export revision that matches nanoda_lib) and make it blocking again. Low priority versus results; do it when a run has spare capacity or before any result is reported as a finding.

## 2026-10-30 · experiment (annex LIV) · truncated-series algebra
- Observation: every d_L/d_Q/lookback theorem so far rebuilt remainder chains by hand (third order took ~10 iterations; one ~25-min heartbeat trap). Change: generic `approx_mul`/`approx_pow` (f ≈ p :⇔ f − p(X) = o(X⁵)), so a series proof is a list of sympy identities `p·r = m + x⁶W` plus one coefficient identity. Effect seen: fifth-order curved d_L(y) compiled after 4 local iterations (~15 min), all errors trivial. How to tell: C7 (d_A) and the z-series (C9) should each take ≤ 3 iterations reusing it.
- Pitfall found: `unfold A B` processes names in order, so a definition introduced by unfolding B is not unfolded by an earlier A; list outer definitions first. PROMPT note.

## 2026-10-30 · experiment (annex LIV) · HasDerivAt combinators must mirror the parse tree
- Problem: 5 failed local compiles in this run came from `exact (combinator).congr_deriv (by ring)` where the combinator's function was not syntactically the target function (`c * f * g` needs `(hf.const_mul c).mul hg`, not `(hf.mul hg).const_mul c`; sums are left-associated). Lean then elaborates `ring` against an unassigned metavariable and reports "ring failed … = ?m". Second source: `HasDerivAt.mul` and friends leave Pi-applications `(f * g) t`, which need `Pi.mul_apply`, `Pi.sub_apply`, `Pi.div_apply` before `ring`.
- Change: PROMPT working note. How to tell: next derivative-chain proof compiles with ≤ 2 such errors.

## 2026-10-31 · run 1028Z · experiment (annex LIV) · meaning carried by prose and names is checked against the statement (trust track T2c; T5 holes A04, B04, A05, D04)
- Problem: every T5 defect still accepted after run 0228Z (A03, A04, A05, B04, D01, D04) carried its false meaning in prose or names: a docstring, a definition's name, a record's label. Run 0828Z made docstrings required, adding a layer that nothing checked.
- Root cause: the checks read statements; the scientist reads names and docstrings; nothing connected the two.
- Change (blocking): `scripts/trust/check_prose.py` + `glossary.txt`, fed by `.lake/meaning.jsonl` from `StatementDeps.lean`: glossary terms in docstrings need a pinned definition the statement reaches; no pinned definition's name inside another definition's name; ∃ conclusions (also through one unfolding step, e.g. `Set.Nonempty`) said in words; pure negations said; no every/any/all/general without universally bound data (after unfolding the program's own claims); every top-level conjunct of a certified-number record that asserts a number reaches a pinned or transcribed definition. Known gaps ratcheted in `prose_baseline.txt` (may only shrink, except new keys named in a LEDGER addition when a strengthened check finds old gaps); `glossary.txt` may only grow.
- Change (blocking): docstrings of headlines and pinned definitions are now part of their frozen fingerprints (a changed plain-words claim needs a LEDGER line like a changed statement). Red team E (Sonnet, this run) showed the need: E02 reversed the sign convention in the docstring of the pinned `decel`, E04 turned a refutation's docstring into an endorsement, E06 generalised a record's docstring.
- Evaluate (next run): judge red team E (`runs/2026-10-31T1028Z/t5/redteamE/`) against the checks on main with judge v6; the expected residue is the class "synonyms and formulas outside the glossary" (E01, E07, E08) and docstrings of *new* headlines (E04/E06 analogues on new constants).

## 2026-11-04 · run 0028Z · integrity event (annex 39.5, addition, not a check) · CI step "Author files for runs without local Lean"
- Problem: this run's environment refused the local Lean install (elan installer: "code from external"). Without local Lean a run cannot regenerate the files a promotion must commit (TRUST_MAP.md, relocked `scripts/trust/frozen.txt`, the validation packet), so Checked promotion was impossible, whatever the proofs.
- Root cause: those files are produced only by `lake env lean scripts/trust/StatementDeps.lean` (+ `make_packet.py`) on the author's machine.
- Change: a final step of the `checked` job (`if: always()`, exit 0, after every blocking step) reruns the trust step with `TRUST_RELOCK=1`, regenerates the packet, prints the files as a base64 tarball between `AUTHOR-FILES-BEGIN/END` and uploads them as the artifact `author-files`. It checks nothing and blocks nothing; no existing step changed. The author still commits the files, and `check_relock.py` still demands a LEDGER line for every changed frozen entry. Control: on a branch with no Checked change (94e22b6, CI 41749090818) the regenerated TRUST_MAP.md, frozen.txt and packet are byte-identical to the committed ones.
- Evaluate (next run without local Lean, or the next promotion): was a promotion completed from the artifact alone, and did any generated file differ from a local regeneration?

## 2026-11-04 · run 0028Z · experiment (annex LIV) · iterate on Lean through CI branch pushes when local Lean is refused
- The Work job of a branch push builds in ≈ 5 min (cache restored from main); the checked job ≈ 20 min. Job status in the Actions API lags; `get_job_logs` returns 404 until the job has finished, then the full log. This run's 9 Work iterations on the branch: 3 failures, each a one-line fix (a `ring` goal blocked by `id x`; an `intervalIntegrable` missing its endpoints; a stuck measure metavariable); the promotion needed 1 branch run of the `checked` job plus the artifact; the main push then failed once on the STATE citation rule, which the branch run had not reached.
- Evaluate: count CI iterations per proved headline next time local Lean is refused.

## 2026-11-04 · run 0228Z · experiment (annex LIV) · second-order certified quadrature replaces monotone Riemann sums
- Problem (pattern): every certified integral so far (B1 runs 0428Z, 0828Z; witnesses) used monotone Riemann sums, first order in the step: 40–80 cells for ≈ 1% accuracy, and per-subinterval constants that multiplied lemmas. The fit-level theorem needed the three distance ratios to 0.85% uniformly in Ω ∈ [0, 1] (referee), out of reach that way.
- Root cause: the enclosure bounded the integrand by its value at a cell end (monotonicity) instead of by a function that can be integrated exactly.
- Change: `Checked/Cosmography/B1FitTools.lean` (`cell_lower`, `cell_upper`, `sum_lower`, `sum_upper`): on each cell, tangent (below) or chord (above) of `1/√y` with `y = Ω + (1−Ω)v⁶`, a polynomial in v integrated exactly; error second order (≈ 1e-4 relative with 20 cells). Generators `runs/2026-11-04T0228Z/scripts/gen_fit.py`, `emit_fit.py` (adaptive Ω-intervals, LP dual certificates), `emit_witness.py`. PROMPT working note added.
- Observed: whole Ω ∈ [0, 1] proof with 9 Ω-intervals, 20 cases, 29 certificate lemmas; the certificate file elaborates in 2.4 min locally; zero Lean iterations failed on the certificates themselves.
- Evaluate (next certified-integral task, e.g. the vdW coexistence curve or B1 with curvature): reuse `sum_lower`/`sum_upper` (any integrand with a convex-in-y outer function and polynomial inner function) and count Lean iterations.

## 2026-11-04 · run 0428Z · T5 red team F (blind) and three new blocking checks (strengthening only)
- Measurement: red team F (Sonnet, brief `runs/2026-11-04T0428Z/t5/briefF.md`, written against 7221e29 = main after run 0228Z; nobody had seen its defects when the checks it attacked were written) produced 8 defects; table `runs/2026-11-04T0428Z/t5/RESULTS.md`.
- Change 1 (StatementDeps.lean, second readings): a theorem in `Checked.Trust.Transcriptions` counts as a second reading (² in TRUST_MAP) only if it states `d = e` with `e` a constant of a `Checked.Trust.SecondReading*` module, `e ≠ d`, and `e`'s statement and body do not reach `d`; any other theorem in that namespace is a blocking error (F06 `agree_dQFlat : dQFlat = dQFlat := rfl`).
- Change 2 (StatementDeps.lean, frozen registry): new entries `doc:<definition>` (docstring of every classified, non-pinned definition, plus the docstrings of the fields of a classified structure) and `moduledoc:<module>` (the `/-! … -/` docs of every Checked module). Existing entries keep their fingerprints (new entries need only the relock). From now on a changed definition docstring, spec-field docstring or module doc needs a LEDGER line naming the definition or module (F01 `critCubic` docstring, F02 `MaxwellSpec` field docs and module doc, F04 `B1Fit` module doc).
- Change 3 (check_numbers.py): code spans are blanked only when they are a constant name or a file path; every other code span is read like prose (F05: a wrong decimal in a code span escaped the check).
- Change 4 (new `scripts/trust/check_trusted_defs.py`, blocking in CI): a "yes"/"is pinned" claim in TRUSTED_DEFINITIONS.md must name a definition and pin theorem that `scripts/trust/pins.txt` lists (F08).
- Not addressed (open, recorded): F03, F04's STATE half (numbers and scope prose in STATE.md; class A03), F07 (a fraction in a FINDINGS heading: fractions are not certified; T4 "not covered yet").
- Evaluate (next red team, G): do the four changes hold against defects aimed at them, and is the frozen-docstring rule a cost for honest promotions (count LEDGER lines it forces)?

- Root cause addressed (annex LIV): four blind red teams in a row (F, G, H, I; 32/32 accepted) put their falsehoods into hand-written prose, mostly docstrings of correct theorems, and each new pattern rule on that prose (check_prose rules 1–7) was beaten by a new construction the next round. Pattern-checking prose cannot close this class; the reader-facing claim has to be produced from the statement.
- New generated file `CLAIMS.md` (`scripts/trust/Claims.lean`, after `lake build Checked`, ≈ 2 min): for each of the 151 headlines, binders as "For every", hypotheses as a numbered "assuming" list, the conclusion (top-level conjuncts numbered), logical connectives in words, formulas printed verbatim by Lean, pinned predicates replaced by their phrase with the actual arguments, and every program definition the statement reaches listed (pinned: phrase + pin theorem; others: kind + full body, Prop bodies rendered in words). The docstring follows, labelled as an unchecked paraphrase; CLAIMS.md states that the generated claim is what was proved. Blocking in CI: stale CLAIMS.md; a pinned definition reached by a headline without a phrase; a phrase placeholder that is not a binder of its definition.
- New registry `scripts/trust/phrases.txt` (22 phrases, one per pinned definition: the only hand-written words in CLAIMS.md). `scripts/trust/check_claims.py` (blocking): a phrase may use a glossary term only if the glossary attributes it to that definition or to a pinned definition it rests on; `--base`: a changed or removed phrase needs a LEDGER line naming the definition.
- README.md and STATE.md banners now point to CLAIMS.md as the machine-generated claim (banner first sentence unchanged; goal guard ok).
- Not yet: the validation packet still shows the docstring as "Claim in plain words" (planted controls would need generated-claim variants); TRUST_MAP rows do not link the claim.
- Experiment: blind red team J (Sonnet) aimed at the generated claims (`runs/2026-11-04T2228Z/t5/briefJ.md`). Measure: how many of 8 defects make a reader of CLAIMS.md believe something false with CI green, versus 8/8 through prose in rounds F–I. Evaluated this run if time allows, else next part-3 run.
- **Experiment evaluated in the same run (blind red team J, Sonnet, 8 defects against 4f60172; `runs/2026-11-04T2228Z/t5/RESULTS.md`):** 1 INVALID (d08, does not compile), 1 CAUGHT (d01, check_prose), **6/7 valid ACCEPTED, all six misleading a reader of CLAIMS.md; in all six the generated text was literally true** (names of unpinned definitions and binders, a `Classical.choose` object, a wrong constant in a FORMULA). - Strengthening after reading J (not blind), all in `scripts/trust/Claims.lean`: leading data binders filling pinned slots are renamed to the slot name unless a decorated form of it (d04, d05 read correctly); omitted proofs printed with their statement, `Classical.choose` objects explained by their property (d02 disclosed); truncating ℕ/ℤ arithmetic printed with numeral types and flagged (d01, d07); decimal constants in non-PRINTED formula bodies flagged (d03 disclosed); **blocking:** two reached definitions with names equal up to primes (d07 REJECTED); unpinned definitions labelled "only the formula shown is checked, not its name" (d06 open). - **New blocking rules (experiment: a later blind round measures them; round K re-judged in `judge_after.txt`):**
  1. `scripts/trust/check_formulas.py --base`: every declaration of any keyword added to, and every change or deletion of, a module under `Checked/Trust/` whose first path part starts with `SecondFormulation`, `SecondReading`, `Formulations` or `Transcriptions` (any depth) needs a LEDGER.md addition naming it and the word "brief" (was: `def` only, SecondFormulation only).

- 2026-11-07, run 2026-11-07T0428Z — **CI workflow change (integrity event, annex 39.5; LIV repair of CI saturation; no check removed or weakened).** Measured: the checked job of 336fca2 (Checked with Thomas–Fermi) spent 5 h 24 min in the nanoda step (23:31–04:55Z) of a 6-hour job limit; the pre-TF job a4c4b03 spent 84 min there, 67 of them in lean4export (104.8 M export lines) and 17 in nanoda; nine runs on main with identical Checked input (tree hash 0f801bfb) ran that step concurrently, and 15 more were queued. Changes in `.github/workflows/lean.yml`: (1) `concurrency: lean-judge-<ref>`, `cancel-in-progress: false` — a running run always finishes, a newer push replaces a queued one; (2) new job `changes` (`scripts/ci_changes.py`, self-tested in CI) computes the base of every base-diff check (check_audits, check_relock and the whole frozen step) as the newest ancestor of HEAD whose whole workflow run concluded `success` (Actions API; fail-closed: no API or no green ancestor ⇒ the run fails) instead of `github.event.before`, so commits whose runs were replaced or cancelled are covered by the next run's diff (the base-diff scripts were run locally over the range a4c4b03..eb787c4: all pass); (3) a push whose tree differs from that green ancestor only in `LOCK` skips the work, checked and nanoda jobs (skipped, not green); (4) nanoda moved from the checked job into its own job `nanoda` (own 6-hour budget; the trust steps no longer wait for it), memoised: a marker is written to the Actions cache only after nanoda passes, keyed by the SHA-256 of every tracked file of Checked/, Checked.lean, lakefile.toml, lake-manifest.json, lean-toolchain, scripts/nanoda_checked.sh and the workflow file; a run on identical input prints the run that passed and does not re-check. Positive controls (39.6) follow on throwaway branches (recorded below when read).

- 2026-11-08, run 2026-11-08T0828Z — **Audit correction of the Homann record (run 2026-11-08T0428Z; records only, no statement changes).** The Homann equation f''' + 2ff'' + 1 − f'² = 0 is the Falkner–Skan equation at β = 1/2 under F(s) = √2 f(s/√2) (now a Checked proof route: `Checked.FalknerSkan.fs_half_exists`); in that normalisation the wall shear is printed to 15 digits (arXiv:1907.09912: 0.927680039836653, read by this run's referee), and the literature calls the β = 1/2 case "Homann flow" (Zhu et al., Table 4; Fazio). So the 0428Z claims "printed only to about four digits" and "first machine-checked enclosure of a constant printed only in floating point" were wrong: the Homann constant is a *certified form of a published number*. Uniqueness: Coppel 1960 (0 < f' < 1) and Craven–Peletier 1972 (for 0 ≤ β ≤ 1 per Fazio arXiv:2011.07725; "0 ≤ β < 1" per the Fluids 6(4):153 review) are reported second-hand to cover β = 1/2 and β = 1 — not read in the original here. Root cause: the 0428Z referee brief asked for printed values of *this* equation only, and the referee saw 0.9277 = 1.3119/√2 without recognising the family. Fix (PROMPT working note, this run): every referee/screen brief for an ODE constant asks whether a scaling maps the equation into a known family and where that family's tables print the constant.
